It should report a reason it can act on, rather than "it is not working inside a git repository". **(6)** Judgement, and the only thing a machine cannot do here: read the reason sentences in `docs/manual/08-changes-and-git.md` and say whether they read as something you could act on, or as jargon — half of them are git's own words quoted back, which is deliberate and may or may not land | Automated: 21 cases driving **real git** against real damaged repositories (a `.git` file pointing nowhere, an unparseable one, a corrupt config, a missing folder, a bare repo), plus stand-ins for the cases real git cannot be made to produce portably. **28 mutants across four rounds, all dead**, including one e2e mutant that proves the sentence reaches glass — nothing else renders that string, so swapping it left 7,758 unit tests green while the fix silently reverted. ⚠️ **Review found a blocker inside the fix, and it is why item 1 is worth doing on a slow or network drive if you have one:** a capability probe that merely *stalled* was remembered as "your git is too old", app-wide, for the life of the process — so one hiccup would have told you to upgrade a git that was fine, on every project, until you restarted. Now reported as "could not check" and forgotten. ⚠️ **CI then found a second one the dev machine could not show:** on Linux a folder that turned out to be a file made the whole read *reject* rather than answer, so the pane would have gone blank instead of explaining. **What no machine covers is item 6** |
| **A backgrounded conversation no longer freezes the Session view (#790)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16) — ready to hand-test.** Open a **Direct** session, send a prompt so it has some history, then use Claude Code's own "push this conversation into the background" gesture inside the card. Expected: the Session view keeps showing the conversation rather than going silent, and **Help → Open log folder** holds `conversation continued elsewhere — rebinding` at info, naming both ids. ⚠️ **The trigger itself is the part nobody has reproduced** — see the note, and treat "I could not make it happen" as a useful result to report, not a failed test | Automated: 9 cases, 14 mutants (13 kill, 1 documented survivor). ⚠️ **The `continued-in` record has never been seen on this machine** — zero across ~3,300 transcripts — and four command-line variants (`--bg`, `--bg --fork-session`, the documented "starts a copy", `claude stop`) each failed to produce one. The shape comes from the CLI binary's own code, not a capture: `spike/findings/e11-790-continued-in.md`. So a hand-test that cannot trigger it confirms the probes rather than contradicting them |
| **Nothing to click, one log line if you insist (#753)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16) — ready to hand-test.** Internal: switchboard now believes Claude Code when it says it cleared a conversation, instead of working it out from the conversation's identifier changing. No screen, button or behaviour changes, and the "Conversation cleared" marker in the feed was never affected — it comes from somewhere else. If you want to see it: open a **Direct** session, quit and relaunch so the session is restored, run `/clear` **before** sending any prompt, then **Help → Open log folder**. You should find `conversation cleared — rebinding to the new transcript` at info. The old line was `transcript mis-bind corrected (same-cwd race)` at **warn**, describing a problem that was not happening. Note this may well have looked right before the change too, for a reason only found in review — see #793 | Automated: 45 cases, 7 mutants (6 kill, 1 documented survivor). The one real bug this cannot cover is **#793**, filed rather than hidden: a hook can land the new id untagged first, and that race is unmeasured |
| **Nothing to click (#779)** | **There is nothing for you to test by hand, and that is the honest answer rather than a gap.** This item only changed switchboard's written record of what Claude Code puts in a session's history file, so that the app stops logging warnings about fields it should already know about. No screen, button or behaviour changes. If you want to see it anyway: **Help → Open log folder**, work a session for a few minutes, and search the log for `schema drift` — you should find nothing. Finding something is genuinely interesting news (a Claude Code release changed the format again) and worth a ticket, not a bug in this change | Automated: 49 cases, 35 mutants, plus two committed probes that measure the whole 246,000-line transcript corpus on this machine and re-run against any later CLI (`spike/probes/779/`). The real gate is `npm run check:transcripts`, which drives the installed CLI and reports drift — it says clean against 2.1.261 |
| **The Changes tab still works, on every project you have (#776)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16) — ready to hand-test.** This is a hardening change with *no new behaviour*, so the whole test is "nothing got worse", and it wants breadth rather than depth: open the **Changes** tab on as many different projects as you can — this repo, BrainHarbor, anything with submodules, anything on a network drive, and **anything using Git Large File Storage if you have one**. Each should list exactly what `git status` lists in a terminal in the same folder, no more and no less. The failure to watch for is an LFS project suddenly showing every large file as modified, or the tab going empty on a project where git works fine from the command line. Then ask one session to look at another session's changes and confirm the diff still comes back | Automated: ~60 cases driving real git — seven ways a repository can try to run a command, on both read paths, each with its own control proving plain git *does* run it, plus submodules, a redirected submodule git directory, and a linked worktree — over 30 more on the argument building, and 27 mutants. What a machine cannot cover is the variety of real repositories on your disks; that is the whole of this row |
| **A waiting-message mark in the sessions rail (#774)** | Open two sessions in different folders. Ask session A to send session B a message. **(1)** Without touching B, its row in the left-hand list should show a **1**; open B and the count goes when you press Enter or dismiss. **(2)** Repeat with B **collapsed**, or sitting on its Terminal tab — the row count should still appear; this is the case the whole item is for. Check the number is legible in your theme and does not crowd the status glyph or make the row jump. **(3)** Put both in a group, collapse the group, send B a message: the heading should read **1 waiting** beside the needs-you summary instead of **calm**. **(4)** Send two or three and confirm the counts climb — and that nothing flashes, no sound plays, and the window does not come forward. That last one is deliberate and is the judgement call worth vetoing if you disagree. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED** — released in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16). |
| **One send at a time from a prompt box (#774)** | Attach a screenshot to a session's prompt box and hammer **Enter** two or three times: it should send once, with the Send button greyed until it lands. | **Sessions can see each other — a "switchboard" MCP server in every session (#763)** | **The one thing to check hardest is the negative: YOUR OWN MCP SERVERS MUST STILL BE THERE.** In a **Direct** session open `/mcp` and confirm you see **DeepWiki** (or whatever you have configured) **and** a new read-only row called **switchboard**, connected. On the work laptop that means your claude.ai connectors too — the laptop is the only machine with a real connector set, so it is the only place the merge is tested at any scale. If ANY server you had is missing, stop and tell me immediately: that is the failure mode this whole item was built to avoid. **(2)** The thing the automation cannot do at all, because it needs a model: in a session, ask *"what other sessions am I running?"* with two or three other sessions open in different folders. It should name them, with their folders and whether each is working or idle — without you telling it the tool exists. **(3)** Check it names the RIGHT ones: it should list your other cards, not itself twice, and the names should be the card titles you see in the rail. **(4)** The read-only row: confirm **switchboard** has no Remove button, and say whether having an unremovable row you did not add is annoying enough to want it hidden instead — that was a judgement call I made rather than asked about, and it is one filter line to reverse. **(5)** Restart a session (**⋯ → Restart**) and confirm the row comes back connected rather than going stale. **(6)** A **Terminal**-mode session: it gets the server too, so `/mcp` typed in the terminal should list it. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now. Automated: 56 new tests. `npm run check:mcp-attach` drives the **real logged-in CLI** and proves the whole chain without a model — our server `connected`, our `serverInfo`, our tool listed, and **DeepWiki still present alongside it**. The eviction flag `--strict-mcp-config` is asserted absent across the whole spawn matrix AND by a source-level guard that fails if the string appears in any shipping file (it evicts every server you configured — measured, three runs). A provider without the capability spawns **byte-identically**, compared against literals. ⚠️ **Review caught a real leak before this shipped:** a session that failed to start (no CLI on PATH, a failed spawn) left a listening socket and a live credential behind for the life of the app — five failed starts, five leaks — and the code comments claimed that could not happen. Fixed with an undo on the failure path, pinned by tests that fail without it. **What no test can judge is items 2, 3 and 4** — whether an agent finds and uses the tool unprompted, and whether the extra row is worth its cost |
| **A session can read another session's recent work and its uncommitted diff (#764)** | **Do the #763 row above first — this builds straight on it, and if the switchboard server is not connected none of this can work.** Setup: two or three sessions open in **different** folders, at least one of them a git repo with uncommitted changes and a conversation with some history in it. **(1)** In session A, ask *"what has \<the other session's name\> been working on?"* — it should go and read, and come back with a recognisable account of that session's recent conversation. Check it against what is actually on that other card. **(2)** The one that pays for the whole item: in A, ask *"look at the uncommitted changes in \<other session\> and tell me if anything looks wrong"* — it should produce a real review of real edits it has never been shown. Compare against `git diff` in that folder. **(3)** Ask about a session that is **not open** ("what has Homebrew been doing?"). It must say **no such session** and list the ones that are — the failure mode to watch for is a confident *"it hasn't done anything"*, which is the answer this item was built to make impossible. **(4)** Two sessions with the **same name** (two checkouts of one repo): asking about that name must report the ambiguity and show both, not silently pick one. **(5)** A **long** conversation: ask for its output and confirm the session tells you it only got the recent part rather than presenting it as the whole story. **(6)** A session whose folder is **not** a git repo: asking for its diff must say *not a repository*, not *no changes*. **(7)** Judge the wording — these strings are read by a model, not by you, so say whether the answers it gives back sound like it understood what it was handed | ⚠️ **UNTESTED — released in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16); this row is ready to run.** Automated: `npm run check:bus` now drives the **real compiled bus server over real pipes against the real query core**, over a real temp git repo and a real transcript — so items 5 and 6 and the caps are machine-proven, and the "unknown session is an error, not an empty success" rule in item 3 is asserted end to end. Plus 40-odd unit tests, five of them mutation-verified by applying the mutant and watching it go red. ⚠️ **One of my own new tests was decoration and the mutant caught it** — it asserted "nothing was logged as an error", which stayed true with the bug in as well as out; it now pins the branch itself. **What no machine can do is any of items 1, 2, 3, 4 or 7, because every one of them needs a model to actually call the tool** — `mcp_status` proves the server is reachable for free, but a tool call costs a turn, so it can never be a CI job. Item 2 is the one to spend time on |
| **Sessions can send each other messages — held in the prompt box until you press Enter (#765)** | **Needs the #763 row working first.** Setup: two **Direct** sessions, A and B, in different folders. **(1)** In A, ask it to *"send B a message asking it to list the files in its folder"*. B's prompt box should show a highlighted block **From @A** with the message — and **B must do nothing**: no reply, still idle. A should say the message is waiting in B's box and has **not** been sent. **This is the safety property; if B acts on its own, stop and tell me.** **(2)** Click into B's box and press **Enter** with nothing typed: B gets it and answers; its conversation shows the message with a header naming A and saying you passed it on. Repeat with some words typed — they should go after the message, in one prompt. **(3)** Send another; click the **×** on the block — it goes away and nothing is sent. **(4)** B's **⋯** → tick **Accept messages from other sessions automatically**; send from A again — B should act straight away, and A should say it was sent. Untick it afterwards. **(5)** The loop check: tell A and B *each* "whenever the other session messages you, reply to it". With the box unticked the exchange must stop at one message sitting in a box. (Optional, costs tokens: tick it on BOTH — it should stop after ~5 each way, with the next message waiting for you.) **(6)** Switch B to its **Terminal** tab (or collapse it), send from A — A should be told B's conversation isn't on screen, and the block should be there when you go back. **(7)** A **Terminal-mode** B with the box ticked: the message should still wait in the box, and A should be told why | ⚠️ **UNTESTED — released in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16); this row is ready to run.** Automated: the non-submission is asserted directly at four levels — the delivery policy (calls to submit counted, never text inspected), the composer (no call to either send route until Enter), `check:bus` through the real compiled bus child and pipe, and an e2e through the real app's wiring. The loop is **exercised**, not argued: two simulated agents that answer every message — one hop with defaults, bounded at 11 sends with both boxes ticked. 18 mutants applied across two review rounds, all went red. ⚠️ **Review found a real hole before this merged:** a message could carry hidden terminal escape codes, so in a Terminal-mode session your ONE Enter on text that looked harmless would have typed extra keys — now refused, along with invisible characters that read differently to a model than to you. **Extra things worth a glance:** a message that appears a split second before you press Enter on your own prompt should stay in the box (it waits one second on screen); and the **Session** tab shows a number while messages wait. **What no machine can do is 1, 4 and 5 with a real model** — whether an agent uses the tool sensibly and heeds "don't wait for a reply", and whether the block reads well |
| **Reading another session never locks its git, and a pile-up of reads is turned away (#772)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.90 (2026-09-16) — ride along with the #764 row, don't do it on its own.** **(1)** While session A is reading B's changes (the #764 row's step 2), have B stage or commit something at the same moment — ask B to *"git add everything and commit it"* right as A starts reading. B's commit must **never** fail with *"index.lock: File exists"*. Hard to hit on purpose; mainly: if you ever see that message in a session while another one is looking at it, report it. **(2)** Optional: ask A to *"use four or five subagents at once to each read what B has been doing"*. Any that are turned away should say to **wait for one of the others to finish** — not "switchboard is unreachable", and the app should not stutter while they run. | Internal-heavy: the index-lock fix, the four-at-once limit, the ten-second git limit and the smaller transcript reads are all pinned by unit tests with their mutants applied, and `check:bus` runs the new diff against a real repo. What only you can judge is (2)'s "does the app stutter" — on the laptop, not the desktop. |
| **Replies are formatted as they arrive, instead of showing raw markdown until the turn ends (#635)** | **This is your report: "it just shows the raw markdown as it scrolls up, and then once it's done, it renders it."** Needs a **Direct** session (Terminal mode waits for whole messages and always did). **(1)** Ask for something long and structured — "explain X with headings, a bulleted list, a table and a code example" — and watch it arrive. Headings, **bold**, bullets and code blocks should be formatted **as they stream**, and there should be **no moment at the end where the whole reply re-flows**. **(2)** The thing you are best placed to judge and no test can: **is the settling distracting?** A word goes plain → bold as the closing `**` lands, and a line becomes a table header only when the row beneath it arrives. Some of that is unavoidable; say if it reads as busy rather than as live. **(3)** Watch a **code block** specifically: the code should appear as it is written, but the **Copy button only appears when the reply finishes** — deliberate, because copying a half-written command would put half a command on your clipboard. Confirm the button then works. **(4)** The block cursor: it should sit at the end of the text while Claude writes, and inside the code box when the reply is mid-fence. **(5)** THE RISKIEST ONE — **scrolling.** With a session streaming a long reply, confirm the view still sticks to the bottom; then scroll **up** mid-reply and confirm it does **not** yank you back down; then `↓ Jump to latest` and confirm it re-sticks. Do it again in a **2×2 split with two sessions replying at once**. **(6)** Performance, on the **work laptop** rather than the desktop: with several sessions replying simultaneously, does typing in the prompt box stay responsive? **(7)** Prose that merely *looks* like markdown must come through untouched — ask for a reply containing `[1]`, `2 ** 8`, `**/*.ts` and `array[0]`; none of it should sprout stray brackets or asterisks | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now. Automated: **all 17 mutations caught**; e2e proves markup renders *while the turn is still running* off deltas that never reach the transcript, and `feed-tail-pin` / `feed-restore-position` stay green. **Both of the ticket's premises were measured and found wrong:** an unclosed code fence needs no auto-closing (`marked` terminates it at EOF), and the cost needs no window cap — median assistant block is **100 characters** and the whole pipeline costs **0.07 ms** on it (corpus: 8,846 transcripts, 19,589 blocks). ⚠️ **Review caught three real bugs, and item 7 plus the dev-mode note are what they became.** The worst: under React StrictMode the block **froze after its first chunk** and only unfroze at end of turn — dev-only, but that is the owner's own environment, and it would have been *worse* than the bug being fixed. Second: the repair mangled ordinary prose (`see the note [1] below` → `see the note [1] below](#)))`) because every test case was a constructed partial and none was plain English — item 7 is that gap. Third: CRLF drifted the token offsets. **What no test can judge is items 2, 5 and 6 — whether the settling is distracting, whether scrolling still feels right, and whether the laptop keeps up** |
| **Click the model name at the bottom of a session to switch it in one click (#747)** | **This is the express lane you asked for, and the thing that pays for the extra click #746 added — judge whether it does.** In a **Direct** session that has replied at least once, click the small grey model name at the bottom, next to the autonomy chip. A menu should open — **upward**, if the card is near the bottom of the window — with a ✓ on the model you are on. Click a different one: it should apply **immediately**, the menu should close, and the name on the button should become the model you picked, with no OK anywhere. **Then the check no machine can make: send a prompt and confirm the reply really comes from that model** — only the real CLI proves `set_model` took effect rather than being merely acknowledged. Second, the placement, which is the reason this does not use the anchoring the ⋯ menu uses (#695): open it on a card in the **top** row of a 2×2 split — the menu should open **downward** and must not cover the button you just clicked — and again on a card at the very bottom, where it should open upward. **Third, the popout, which is the case most likely to be wrong:** pop a session out with **⤢**, click the model name in the popped-out window, and confirm the menu appears **in that window**, anchored to that button, and switches **that** session. Fourth, the fresh card: open a brand-new session that has not replied and confirm the button reads **model?**, opens anyway, ticks nothing, and explains why — then pick one and confirm it takes. Fifth, the one that must NOT be a button: switch a session to **Terminal** mode from **⋯** and confirm the model name there is **plain text**, with a tooltip pointing at `/model` in the Terminal tab. Do the same on a **stopped** session — also plain text, and its tooltip must NOT mention a Terminal tab it never had. Finally the feel: `Esc`, clicking away, `Tab`, and clicking the button a second time should all just close it, and the arrow keys should walk the rows. **Say whether one click with no confirmation is right here, given the picker deliberately asks** | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now. Automated: 32 tests, **all 25 mutations caught**. Pinned — one click is exactly one `set_model` carrying the model's id rather than its display name; clicking the model it already runs sends **nothing** (a no-op switch would make "it did something" indistinguishable from "it didn't"); a refusal **holds the menu open** in the CLI's own words with nothing moved; exactly one row ticks on the default model (`default` and `opus[1m]` share a resolved id — a per-row test lights both); the menu never asks main which model is current, because the button already knows; Terminal mode and a dead session both keep the plain span. ⚠️ **Review caught two blockers before this shipped, and they are what items 3 and 5 are really checking.** First, the button was a way to tear the menu down **while a switch was still on the wire** — reachable by keyboard — which would have lost the CLI's refusal silently and left the session on the old model while you believed it had changed. It now goes dead, visibly, until the answer lands. Second, a session **ending or restarting** under an open menu left the menu floating, aimed at a session id that no longer resolved. **What no test can judge is the feel of one-click-no-confirm, or whether the menu lands in the right place on a real screen** — items 1, 2, 3 and the last one |
| **The model name at the bottom of a session updates the moment you switch (#746 part 1)** | **This is the bug you reported on 2026-09-03.** Open a session that has replied at least once (so it has a model to show — a brand-new card has not told anyone yet, which is by design), note the small grey model name along the bottom, then `/model`, pick a different one, press OK. **The name at the bottom must change immediately** — not on the session's next reply, which is what it used to wait for. Then the second half of the same claim: open a session that has been running a while and confirm the name is there at all, without you switching anything. Third, the one that would be a regression rather than a miss: the **cost** figure and token counts must still behave exactly as before — those are deliberately still fed from the conversation itself, not from the picker, because they are about what actually ran | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now. Automated: the footer moves on the push and does so without a new turn (mutation-verified — putting the old wiring back shows **the stale model winning over the new one**); a card mounting mid-session pulls the model it missed; a session that changes nothing announces nothing (the init arrives every turn, so an unconditional push would be one message and one repaint per turn per session); a refused switch pushes **nothing**; and a usage snapshot can no longer overwrite a model the picker has reported, which is the bug itself. Merged 2026-09-03 as PR #750 (`28ef273`). ⚠️ **Review caught a blocker before this shipped, and it is the thing to check hardest:** the first cut read ONLY the picker's source, which would have made the model name **vanish entirely** on Terminal-mode sessions and on any session that had not replied yet — the picker's source is fed only by Direct-mode traffic. So please confirm the name is present on a **Terminal-mode** session too, and on a **resumed** card the moment it appears |
| **The model picker asks you to confirm — select, then OK (#746 part 2)** | **You asked for this shape; only you can say whether it lands.** `/model`, click a model — it should mark your choice and **nothing should happen yet**. Press **Cancel**: the session must be exactly as it was, no switch, nothing to undo. Do it again and press **Esc**, then again and click outside the dialog — all three must behave identically to Cancel. Then click a model and press **OK**: it switches and the dialog closes. **Judge the trade honestly**: this is one more click than before on a thing you may do often, which is exactly why the footer quick-switch (#747) exists as the express lane — say whether the dialog now feels too slow on its own, and whether closing-on-success is enough confirmation or you want a message first. Also check the small stuff: OK should be dead until you have actually chosen something different, and picking the model the session is already on should leave it dead | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now. Automated: a row click sends nothing (mutation-verified — restoring apply-on-click puts it back on the wire and reddens five tests), OK sends exactly once and sends the model's id rather than its display name, Cancel/Esc/click-away all send nothing, OK is disabled until the choice would change something, a refusal keeps the dialog open in the CLI's own words with the selection intact, and a second press cannot put two switches on the wire. **What no test can judge is whether the extra click is worth it** — that is the whole of item 1 |
| **Opening a session with a long history no longer freezes the app (#742, bug 2 of #719's 5)** | **You need a genuinely big conversation for this — a short one never showed the problem.** Open a session on a folder whose conversation has thousands of messages in it (this repo and BrainHarbor both qualify), and watch the moment it picks the conversation up. Before this, the whole app went unresponsive for about a quarter of a second as it swallowed the file: terminals stopped printing, the window stopped repainting. It should now come up without any stall you can feel. **The regression that would matter most is the one to check hardest: the conversation must still come back COMPLETE and from the beginning.** Scroll right up to the top of that session and confirm the history is all there — that replay is deliberate (it's what fixed resumed cards showing an empty Session view) and this change was only allowed to alter *when* the lines arrive, never which ones. Second, open several big sessions at once, or relaunch with a workspace full of them, and confirm the app stays usable the whole way through rather than freezing once per session. Third, and this one is a judgement call only you can make: a very long history now fills in over a few hundred milliseconds instead of appearing all at once — say whether that reads as the app working or as the app being slow. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08), merged as PR #749 (`958ac42`) — installable now. Automated: 12 tests, every one mutation-verified red first, with the defect's own numbers — putting the bug back reads all 200 lines inside a single tick and the event loop gets **zero** turns across the whole replay. Also pinned: the sliced replay produces a byte-identical snapshot to the unsliced one; a multi-byte character (é / 日本語 / 😀) split across a slice boundary is not corrupted; a JSON line split across a boundary parses exactly once; a poll tick landing mid-replay neither duplicates nor skips (removing the guard reads 24 lines where 4 were held); a session that dies mid-replay still gets its last words (removing that reads **6 lines of 100**); and a stopped watcher runs no further slices. **Measured, not estimated: 269.5 ms of frozen main thread on the 33.22 MB / 11,295-line transcript, and that figure excludes the per-line work entirely.** ⚠️ **Known limit, deliberate:** a session that dies *while* its history is still loading still pays the old freeze once, because the alternative is discarding transcript you can never get back |
| **switchboard stops using more and more CPU the longer it's open (#719, bugs 1+3 of 5)** | **This is the row for the incident that cost you a forced reboot.** It cannot be checked in a minute — the whole defect was that cost grew with UPTIME, so the test is *leave it running*. On the work laptop, open your usual set of sessions and **leave switchboard up for a full working day**, ideally longer, then look at its CPU in Task Manager while sessions are idle. It should sit near nothing. **The specific thing to watch for is the shape from the report: CPU that is unremarkable for the first few hours and creeps up over a day**, and the app burning hard while MINIMIZED. Both should be gone. Second check, cheaper: with several sessions actively replying at once, watch CPU during the replies — it should not spike in proportion to how many conversation files are being written. Third, and this is the regression that would matter most: **start a brand-new session and confirm it still finds its conversation as fast as it always did** — that is the thing this change traded against, and if a session now takes visibly longer to come alive, say so immediately. **Two caveats.** This is 2 of the 5 bugs in that report — the other three are filed (#742, #743, #744) and not fixed, so a recurrence is not a surprise and IS worth telling me about. And **the spinning line was never found**: this removes a large amount of measured pointless work, which is not the same as proving it was the cause. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.7** (2026-09-01) — installable now. Automated: the discovery ladder now climbs through an append storm (mutation-verified — putting the defect back gives **40 sweeps per 10 seconds instead of 6**), a busy turn costs a bounded number of sweeps, the conversation-head memo is not re-read every sweep (mutation-verified — **64 quarter-megabyte reads where there should be 0**), and the memo invalidates when a file gains its first identifying line (mutation-verified — keying it by name alone means a session **never binds at all**, which would be a worse bug than the one being fixed). The fail-open fence — a brand-new conversation file is still noticed immediately — is asserted and passed before AND after, deliberately. **What no test can do is run for 26 hours on your laptop**, which is the entire claim |
| **Typing in the prompt box lags less (#716) — HALF a fix** | ⚠️ **EXPECT THIS TO BE BETTER, NOT FIXED, AND SAY WHICH.** Only the composer half shipped; the feed half was built, failed CI and was reverted (see the note), so the honest prediction is: **clearly better on the desktop, possibly still heavy on the laptop in a long session.** Your answer decides whether #716 closes. Open the **longest** session you have — this does nothing measurable on a short one, because the cost it removes was priced by conversation length. Type a couple of sentences at your normal speed and say whether characters appear **as you press them**. Then: (1) with a **long draft** already in the box — paste a paragraph or two so the box is at its twelve-line cap, then keep typing at the bottom of it; (2) while the session is **actively working**; (3) scrolled **up** into the history rather than at the tail. Then confirm the box itself still behaves: it should still grow as you type, still stop at twelve lines and scroll inside itself, still shrink back when you delete, and still give the conversation its room back. **Typing at the bottom of a full box must not jump you to the top of your own draft** — that hazard is now structurally impossible rather than guarded against, so if you see it, something is badly wrong. Finally, on the desktop, compare a long session against a fresh one: typing should now feel the **same** in both, which is the specific thing that changed. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED and deliberately INCOMPLETE. RELEASED in v0.8.7** (2026-09-01) — installable now, and this row is finally answerable. ⚠️ **2026-08-31, owner, work laptop: typing still sluggish — but he was on v0.8.6, which does NOT contain this fix. That is a BASELINE confirmation, not a test of it.** Do not read it as "the fix didn't work"; the fix was not present. **Install v0.8.7 first, then re-run this row** — his answer is what decides whether #716 closes or waits for #740. Automated: the arithmetic that decides where the box stops, the structural guard that typing reads no layout and writes no height (the regression that would bring the lag straight back), and — unchanged on purpose, as the evidence behaviour survived — the real-engine e2e that grows the box with wrapped text, caps it at twelve lines on both branches, shrinks it back and re-fits it on window shrink. **Measured at 400 turns with a long draft: unthrottled, median keystroke latency 80ms → 40ms and the stalls went 58 → 0 — typing now costs what an EMPTY conversation costs. Under a 4× CPU throttle (the laptop proxy) there is NO measured improvement: 152ms/key either way.** The reverted feed half took that to 56ms and 0 stalls, and is what the follow-up item is for. **So the laptop is the real question and only you can answer it** — if it still bursts for you, that is expected rather than a surprise, and it means the follow-up has to land before #716 closes. **ANSWERED 2026-09-16, owner, work laptop on v0.8.8 (contains the composer half): STILL BURSTS** — ~10 characters flush at once, then a pause while he keeps typing. The expected incomplete-fix outcome, now confirmed on real hardware: **#740 (the feed half) is the operative ticket; #716 stays open until it lands.** Both issues annotated same day |
| **Auto-trust actually applies now (#724)** | Set the title-bar chip to **🔓 auto-trust** (the default), then start a **Terminal**-mode session in a folder Claude Code has never run in — auto-trust only has a visible effect there, because Direct mode never gets asked the trust question at all. You should go straight to the composer with **no trust prompt**. **The case this fixes needs a lower-case drive letter**, which Windows produces readily: open a folder by dragging it in from Explorer, or via a shell integration, rather than through the folder picker — before this, that wrote the trust flag under `c:/...` while Claude Code read `C:/...`, so the prompt appeared anyway and kept appearing on every later run. Do it twice on the same folder: the second run is where the old bug was worst, because our own phantom entry made switchboard think it had already succeeded. **Then the diagnostic one:** this repo is one of five folders on the dev desktop that already has TWO entries in `~/.claude.json` under different drive-letter cases. Run a Terminal session here and check the log for `folder has more than one entry in the CLI config` — it should appear once, with both spellings named, and both should end up trusted. | ⚠️ **UNTESTED. RELEASED in v0.8.7** (2026-09-01) — on `main` as #738 (`f4ff920`), installable now. v0.8.6 did NOT contain it, which is why this row could not be run before today. Automated: the key lookup (reuse an existing spelling, invent one only when nothing matches, never add a third), the second-run trap, the array/malformed-config guards, and — the one review caught — that two case-different spellings are confirmed by RESOLUTION rather than spelling, because a `//wsl.localhost/...` UNC path is a win32 path over a case-SENSITIVE backend and trusting both would hand the hooks-enabling flag to a directory you never opened. Measured on this machine; the live config already carries such a key. **What no test can do is prove the real `claude` binary skips its trust prompt on your disk** — that is items 1 and 2. |
| **Sign in to an MCP connector, and sign out again (#734)** | ⚠️ **THE WORK LAPTOP IS THE ONLY MACHINE THAT CAN TEST THIS AT ALL.** The dev machine has no claude.ai connectors, so the entire success path was built without ever being seen — only the CLI's *refusals* are measured. On the laptop, open `/mcp` in a **Direct** session and find a connector row. If any row says **needs sign-in**, it should now have a **Sign in** button beside it — before this it said the words and offered nothing. Click it and report what actually happens: does a browser window open? **Then leave the panel open and watch the row for a full minute** — it keeps re-checking for about that long and should update itself when the sign-in lands, without you closing anything. Say whether it actually does. **Three specific outcomes worth telling apart, because we could not:** (a) it works and the row changes — say how long it took; (b) you get *"Claude Code hasn't answered yet…"* after about ten seconds — that is the control channel's timeout, it is EXPECTED if Claude Code waits for the browser flow, and the question is whether the row still updates afterwards; (c) nothing happens at all — a real result, worth a ticket, not you doing it wrong. Next, **Sign out**, which only appears on rows that are already not working (needs-sign-in, not-connecting, unknown) — never on a connected one, deliberately, because we cannot tell an OAuth connector from a server using an API-key header and signing the wrong one out would break it with no proven way back. Confirm it really does clear the sign-in rather than just saying so. Then the negative case, which IS measured: a stdio row (`claude mcp add selftest -- claude mcp serve`, then reopen) must show **neither** button, because the CLI refuses both by type. Finally the clutter judgement: a row can now carry Sign in, Sign out, Turn off and Reconnect at once — say whether that is too many. Clean up with `claude mcp remove selftest -s local` | ⛔ **UNTESTED — and the 2026-08-31 laptop trip did NOT cover it either.** Dan confirmed the panel lists every server; he did not click Sign in or Sign out. Still untestable anywhere but the laptop, and still the only path in the app whose success case has never once run. **RELEASED in v0.8.6** (2026-08-30), as #736. Automated: the transport reader (including the regression that started this — an absent `config` must report `unknown`, never `stdio`, or every connector gets labelled as the one transport that cannot authenticate), the full sign-in/sign-out rule table, both IPC gates on both channels, the request builders, the post-sign-in watch window (mutation-checked — review caught the panel promising a self-updating list it did not actually deliver), and — the one that matters most — an explicit test that `ok: true` is **never** rendered as "Signed in", because that payload has never been observed. **What no test here can do is exercise a single real OAuth flow.** Outcomes (a), (b) and (c) in the how-to-test column are that gap, and (b) in particular is a guess about the CLI's own behaviour that only your laptop can settle |

A panel appears above the prompt box with the question and clickable answers: round buttons = pick one, boxes = pick several, always an **Other** with a text field. Answer, hit **Send answer**, and Claude should carry on using what you chose — including free text typed into Other. Try the arrow keys/Space instead of the mouse. Half-answer it, go to **Changes**, come back — your ticks should still be there. Unfocused: the pop-up says "A question for you: …" and has **no Allow button**. Note a **Terminal**-mode session keeps its questions in the terminal by design | **v0.7.0.** The fake-provider path is e2e-covered; only the real CLI proves the model actually *reaches for* the tool, and only you can judge whether Other feels like a first-class answer |
| **Several questions arrive as tabs (#566)** | Get a Direct session to ask you **two or more** questions in one go ("ask me three separate questions before you start"). They should arrive as tabs across the top of the question panel, labelled with Claude's own short names, one question showing at a time. Check: every tab carries ✓ or ○ for answered/unanswered; with nothing answered **Send answer** is dead and the panel says "Still to answer: …" naming the ones still empty (it goes live at one answer since #567 — see the row below); **Left/Right** move between tabs and **Up/Down** stay inside the question you are on; half-answer it, go to **Changes** and come back — it should reopen on the question still missing an answer, with your ticks intact. Then ask ONE question and confirm there are no tabs at all | Not yet released — on main after #566. The fake-provider path is e2e-covered; only the real CLI proves a multi-question call happens naturally and that the headers read well as tab names |
| **Answer only SOME of Claude's questions (#567)** | Get a Direct session to ask you **two or more** questions at once, then answer only ONE of them. **Send answer** must go live at that first answer. Before you send, check you can see what you are leaving out: the unanswered tab goes **dashed and struck through**, the question itself (click its tab) says *"Not answered — will be sent as skipped"*, and the line beside the button reads *"Sending now skips: …"* rather than *"Still to answer: …"*. Send it, and watch what Claude does with the gap — it should treat that question as skipped (usually saying so and offering to ask again), never as though you answered it with silence. Then confirm the floor: with NOTHING answered, **Send answer** stays dead — a blank answer is what **Don't answer** is for | Not yet released — on main after #567. The CLI's reading of a partial answer is MEASURED (probe, 2026-08-19, findings §3a) and the partial round trip is e2e-covered against the fake; only the real CLI shows you how Claude phrases the gap, and only you can judge whether the skip warnings read as clear rather than as a telling-off |
| **Feeds restore at the tail (#555)** | Scroll to the end of a busy session, quit switchboard, relaunch. It should come back showing the END of the conversation, not the top. Then drag the panel to another part of the workspace — it should keep its position rather than jumping | **v0.7.0.** Was: every session restored scrolled to the top |
| **Find is a bar first (#557)** | Ctrl+F in a session — you should get the bar with a match count, and Enter / Shift+Enter walking the hits. The results list should stay shut behind its **▸** until you click it | **v0.7.0** |
| **Find in a RESUMED session (#496 + #495)** | Resume a session, send one new turn, then Ctrl+F for something that appears both before and after the resume. Post-resume hits should jump normally; only the older hydrated ones stay unjumpable, and the notice should be about that one hit rather than over the whole session | **v0.7.0.** The ticket's premise was measured wrong first — see PROGRESS |
| **File menu: Open File / Exit (#569)** | A **File** menu at the top left. **Open File…** browses — it should start in the folder of the session you are looking at, and after that wherever you last browsed. The file opens in a document tab BESIDE that session, in its dock section, never inside the session’s own tab strip. Try it with two sessions docked side by side and the SECOND one focused: the document must appear next to that one. Also try it right after typing a prompt (composer focused) — it must still open. **⚠ TEST THIS ONE FIRST (#521).** This row going untested is not a formality: the owner filed #521 (*"no discoverable way to open a file"*) against v0.4.0 before this menu existed, hand-tested only the Ctrl+O half on 2026-08-20 (the row below), and the menu itself was never opened — so the report stayed open for six weeks after the thing it asked for had shipped. An automated guard that the menu bar is actually ON SCREEN landed with #521's layer 1; what a machine still cannot check is whether you can FIND it | **v0.8.0.** |
| **Ctrl+O, and the CLI’s own ctrl+o (#569)** | **Ctrl+O** anywhere in the app opens the file browser — including while typing a prompt. **But click into a session’s Terminal tab and press Ctrl+O there: it must reach Claude Code** (its transcript view), NOT open our dialog. That is the P7 line, and it is the one check no automated test in this repo can make | **TESTED (app half) 2026-08-20:** Dan pressed Ctrl+O in a (Direct) session — dialog opened to that session's folder, as designed. **Terminal-pane half MOOT** while terminals are unused (owner, 2026-08-20); revive with PTY use |
| **No empty window after a restart (#494)** | Open a file in the viewer (the ↗ beside a changed file), pop it into its own window with the ↗ in the document header, then **quit switchboard while that window is still open** and start it again. You should get exactly ONE window back — the document viewer is not restored (by design, for now) and neither is the empty window it was in. Then do it again with a **session** popped out into that same window: that window MUST come back, with its session in it | **0.8.1.** Was: an empty second window could be left on screen, depending on machine timing |
| **Popped-out window comes forward (#571)** | Pop a session out, put another window in front of it, then click that session's row in the sidebar — its window should come to the front. Then click the main switchboard window: your popped-out windows must NOT come forward with it | **v0.8.0** |
| **Changes tab remembers your file (#562)** | Open **Changes** on a session, pick a file, scroll into the diff. Switch to **Session**, switch back — same file, same line. Then commit or discard that file and come back: the tab should open CLEAN, not on a blank comparison | **v0.8.0** |
| **Documents keep their place (#562)** | Open two files in the viewer (the ↗ beside a changed file). Read halfway down one, click the other tab, click back — you should still be halfway down | **v0.8.0** |
| **[UNRESOLVED] Terminal scrollback across a panel move (#562)** | Needs a session with real scrollback: run something long in the **Terminal**, scroll UP into the output, then click that card’s row in the sidebar (with two sessions docked side by side) and see whether the terminal keeps its position. The automated harness could not fill one screen, so this is the one panel measured only halfway | **MOOT** while terminals are unused (owner, 2026-08-20). If PTY sessions return: if it jumps to the bottom it needs the same fix the document viewer got |
| **Right-click menu fits a short window (train fix)** | Shrink the switchboard window to a short height (or use a small screen), right-click a session row near the bottom — the menu should flip above the pointer or clamp to the edge, with every item reachable; a menu taller than the window scrolls | Not yet released — lands with the 2026-08-20 train |
| **A visible ✕ on the Events drawer (#556)** | Open the drawer (tab, `Ctrl+E`, or the palette). There should be a **✕** at the top right of the drawer, beside the word EVENTS — the thing you went hunting for. Click it: the drawer closes and your cursor goes back where it was, exactly as `Esc` does. Then open it again and press **Tab** once — focus should land on that ✕ | Not yet released — on main after #556 |
| **Close all documents + honest tab ✕ (#543)** | Open four or five files in the viewer, then `Ctrl+Shift+P` → **Close all documents (keeps popped-out ones)**. Every document tab should vanish at once, with no confirmation, and your sessions and their **Changes** tabs must be untouched. Then repeat with one document popped out to another monitor (⤢ in its header): that window must survive, and if it is the ONLY document open the palette entry should be greyed out saying "No documents to close". Also hover a document tab's ✕ — the tooltip must read **Close document**, not "Close (ends the session)", and clicking it must not ask you to confirm | Not yet released — on main after #543. Automated tests cover the docked close-all and the three tooltip strings; the popped-out window is unit-pinned only |
| **An attached file survives a tab switch (#546)** | In a **Direct** session, paste a screenshot into the prompt box and type NOTHING. Switch that card to **Terminal**, then back to **Session** — the chip must still be there and the send button still lit. Repeat with a file dragged in from Explorer, and with words typed beside it. Then quit switchboard with a file attached and relaunch: your **words** come back, the chip does **not**, and a line under the box should read "Not restored: <the file name>…". That last part is the point — it must never just empty itself | Not yet released — on main after #546. Automated tests cover the remount and the relaunch notice against the fake provider; only a hand-test proves a real pasted screenshot behaves |
| **Permission-mode hover copy (#534)** | Hover the 🛡 chip in the **title bar**, the 🛡 button **under the prompt box**, and the small mode marker in a **card's header** (visible once a session is on anything but `ask`). Each should pop a description of the mode plus a line saying what that particular control does — and the three must agree. Cycle to **full-auto** and read it: it must say `bypassPermissions` / `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, not imply guardrails. Judge whether the copy reads like something a person would want on hover, or is too long | Not yet released — on main after #534. Unit + component tests already pin that all three surfaces carry the same text and that the full-auto wording matches the CLI; only you can judge readability and hover feel |
| **`ask` mode actually asks (#587)** | Start a session at **ask** and open its **Terminal** tab. The CLI's status line should say **`⏸ manual mode on`** — before this fix a Pro/Max account on CLI 2.1.228+ would have shown **`⏵⏵ auto mode on`** instead, meaning a classifier was reviewing actions rather than you. Then check nothing got taken away: **Shift+Tab** inside that terminal must still cycle to auto. Repeat on a **Direct** session (there is no status line there — the check is only that it still starts, resumes and holds normally). Watch for whether `ask` now prompts noticeably more than it used to; a few more is the fix working, a flood is worth a ticket | Not yet released — on main after #587. No automated test can read the CLI's own status line, so this row is the only proof the flag landed where it counts |
| **Maximizing a suspended session (#216)** | Open two sessions. Pop one out into its own window, then close that window with its **X** — the card comes home showing **Session suspended**. It should now have a proper header (name, colour dot/badge, the word *suspended*). **Double-click that header**: the workspace should fill with that one card and the other session should fold into the Collapsed strip. Double-click again to put it back. Through all of it the card must stay suspended — the **Resume** button still there, nothing restarted | Not yet released — on main after #216 |
| **A pinned session stops scrolling away (#295)** | Open enough sessions that the Sessions list has to scroll (or shrink the window until it does). Pin one near the BOTTOM — it jumps to the top, as before. Now scroll the list to the end: the pinned row must stay parked at the top while everything else slides underneath it. Pin a second one and check the two park as a pair rather than overlapping. Tab through the list from the top and confirm the highlight never lands on a row hidden behind the pinned block. Then the judgement call: with GROUPS, a pinned session only stays put while its group card is on screen — scroll past the whole group and it goes with it. Decide whether that reads as sensible or as the promise half-kept | **v0.8.3.** Automated: an overflowing rail, scrolled to its end, is e2e-checked to still show the pinned row, and the keyboard guard with it. Only your eye judges whether a floating row without its group header reads right, and whether it needs a shadow or a divider to separate it from the rows sliding under |
| **A dead card that finally has a header (#606)** | Get a card into the **Session didn't start** state — easiest is to open a session on a folder, quit, rename that folder, and relaunch. That card should now carry a header like every other card: the session's name, its colour and badge, and the words **not started**. Before this it had no header at all. Check it has no buttons of its own (**Try again** and **Close** stay in the panel below), and that double-clicking the header does not start anything. Worth knowing, and worth your opinion: that card still has NO ROW in the Sessions list, because the session was never registered — so double-clicking its header cannot maximize it the way a live or suspended card's can. Tell me if the missing row bothers you more than the missing maximize | **v0.8.3.** Automated: unit + e2e cover the header, the identity, the pill word, the absence of controls, the maximize round trip through the store, and that nothing respawns. Only your eye judges whether a card with nothing running in it wearing a header reads as informative or as a card pretending to be alive |
| **The app says when it moved a card's conversation (#539)** | On your **next launch after this ships**, open the Events drawer (the tab on the right edge, `Ctrl+E`). Expect a notice saying **Switchboard.ai-2** and **Switchboard.ai** were both pointing at one conversation, that **Switchboard.ai** kept it and Switchboard.ai-2 starts a new one — plus, if the sweep reattached an orphaned card, a second notice naming that card. Then open both: **Switchboard.ai** must be in the conversation it was already in, and **Switchboard.ai-2** must be EMPTY (a new conversation) — never the same transcript as its twin, and never some unrelated session's history. Now the part that matters most: quit **without** pressing Got it and relaunch — the notices must still be there. Press **Got it** on each, relaunch again: gone for good, and both cards land where they were left | Not yet released — on main after #539. Automated tests cover the policy, the persistence, the dismissal and the notice UI; only your real workspace has the actual duplicate pair |
| **Reorder the Sessions list (#559)** | Open three sessions and drag one onto the top half of another — a thin line should show where it lands, and letting go should put it there. Quit and relaunch: the order must come back exactly as you left it. Then right-click a row → **Move up** / **Move down** under *Order in this group*, and try **`Ctrl+Alt+↑`** / **`Ctrl+Alt+↓`** while you are in a session. Now **pin** the top session and try to drag another above it — it should stop just underneath, and **Move up** should go grey there. Dragging onto a *different* group must still just move it there, at the bottom | Not yet released — on main after #559. Automated: the drag + relaunch round-trip and the menu path are e2e; only your eye judges whether the insertion line reads right mid-drag |
| **Find works on a Terminal you never opened (#517)** | Start a fresh session in **Terminal** mode and let it print something, WITHOUT clicking the Terminal tab. From the Session tab press **Ctrl+F** and search for a word you know it printed: you should get a real count in the **Terminal (scrollback only)** group, plus a line saying to open the Terminal tab if you want to scroll to them. Now open the Terminal tab and search again — the same matches should highlight in place and step with Enter. Also check a **Direct** session still shows no Terminal group at all | Not yet released — on main after #517 |
| **The Events drawer (0.6.0)** | The 220px column is gone — look for the slim badge tab on the right edge; it shows the attention count, tinted by the hottest event. Click it, or the hotkey, or the palette; the drawer overlays the grid. Check the update/reconnect/incident notices appear inside it, Esc closes and gives focus back, and the status bar now carries a queue count | Shipped in v0.6.0 |
| **Quiet hours (0.6.0)** | Palette → quiet hours (or the About button); set a window covering now; a session needing you must NOT sound/speak/toast — but a webhook still fires | Shipped in v0.6.0 |
| Actionable toasts (Allow/Deny on Windows notification) | Unfocus the app, make a session hit a permission ask; buttons on the toast must work without focusing the app; answering in-app withdraws the toast | Dan got NO toast at all 2026-08-15 — possibly OS focus assist / notification settings during dev; INVESTIGATE before re-test (installed build should have AppUserModelId) |
| Notify-when-done (⋯ menu tick-box) | Tick one session, task it, alt-tab; toast on finish for that session only; survives restart | Same no-toast caveat as above |
| Phone push (ntfy/Pushover) + webhook | Palette → "phone push" → paste an ntfy topic → Send test; then a real needs-permission while unfocused | Deferred by Dan; needs 2-min setup |
| Themes under the new UI | High-contrast + daylight, re-run find/copy/chips/jump-latest checks | Deferred by Dan |
| Sounds 🔊 / TTS announce 🗣 | Title-bar toggles; per-session cue from ⋯ menu; announce speaks label while unfocused | Shipped 0.5.0, not yet exercised |
| Webhook payload | POST target you own; fires regardless of focus, includes finished turns | Never exercised |
| Service-health incident path | Needs a real/mocked Anthropic incident — dot, Events entry, corroboration banner | Hard to test on demand; low priority |

## Re-test after a fix ships

| What | Shipped in | Re-test |
|---|---|---|
| **"1 need you" while every row reads Done (#1137)** | **IN v0.8.116 (2026-10-08) (PR #1148) — installable now.** | **Your 2026-10-07 report, reproduced before it was fixed.** A finished session you had already looked at left the count but kept its highlight, so the heading said 1 over rows that all looked the same. **(1)** THE MAIN ONE: let three sessions in one group finish while you are looking at something else. The heading says **3 need you** and all three rows are highlighted. Click one: the heading says **2 need you** and that row goes plain, still reading **done**. **(2)** Click the other two: the heading says **calm** and no row is highlighted. **(3)** The lamps across the top match: a filled lamp for each session being counted, hollow for the rest, and the number beside them equals the filled ones. **(4)** A JUDGMENT CALL TO CHECK: get a session asking for permission, open **Events** and **✕** its entry without answering. The count drops and the row loses its highlight, but the word on the row stays colored, because it is still waiting. Say if you would rather that row stayed highlighted. |
| **A session "went back" after the update: its newest text was missing (#1140)** | **IN v0.8.116 (2026-10-08) (PR #1146) — installable now.** | **Your 2026-10-07 report (Tristana3, after updating to 0.8.115), reproduced before it was fixed.** Nothing was lost: a restart was putting every subagent's old activity at the BOTTOM of the conversation, under the newest reply. **TO CONFIRM THIS IS WHAT YOU SAW, on 0.8.115, before updating:** open Tristana3 and scroll UP past the block of subagent activity at the bottom; the missing text should be sitting above it. If it is not there at all, say so, because then this fix is not your bug. **After updating: (1)** THE MAIN ONE: pick a session that has used subagents (an **Agent** call in its conversation), quit the app and start it again. Its conversation must end on the last thing it said, not on subagent activity. **(2)** Scroll up to where that session ran a subagent: the subagent's activity sits there, under its **Subagent** caption, between the request and the result. **(3)** Give that session a new prompt that uses a subagent: its activity appears live, as before. |
| **The 🕘 list of previous conversations was full of things that are not conversations (#1088)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1128) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | **Your report: "It lists all sorts of crap in there."** Most of it was the app's own background requests for task labels, saved as conversations. **(1)** Click **🕘** on a session in a folder you use a lot: no rows beginning "Below is the recent transcript of a coding session", and none that say only `/clear`. **(2)** A session you cleared and then started with a command reads as that command (`/startup …`), not `/clear`. **(3)** Your real conversations are all still there — say so if one you expect is missing. **(4)** Leave the app running with AI labels on for a day, then look again: no new junk rows. **(5)** In a terminal, `claude --resume` in the same folder: no new label rows there either (old ones remain; only the app's list hides them). |
| **White scrollbar in dark dialogs (#1104), and the Events tab over the Files tab's git letters (#1105)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1125) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | Found in the manual's own pictures. **(1)** In a dark theme, open **Settings** (Ctrl+,) and the command palette (Ctrl+Shift+P): the scrollbar is dark. **(2)** Switch to **daylight**: it is light again. **(3)** Look around a dark theme for anything else the system draws — checkboxes, drop-downs — that now looks wrong; this change affects those too. **(4)** **Files** tab in a repo with changes: every M and U at the end of a row is readable, including the rows level with the **EVENTS** tab. |
| **Typing lags while Claude is replying (#716, #1013) — THREE measured steps now** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1123, then the second step the same day, PR #1131; the third on 2026-10-07, PR #1133) — IN v0.8.115, installable now.** Both issues stay OPEN until you say so on the laptop | **Your 2026-10-05 report.** Each piece of a streaming reply was redrawing the whole conversation. **BEST TESTED ON THE LAPTOP.** **(1)** Open a session with a long history, ask for a long written reply, and TYPE IN THE PROMPT BOX WHILE IT STREAMS: letters should appear as you type, not in bursts. **(2)** Same with two or three sessions replying at once. **(3)** While a reply streams: scroll up, expand a tool box, Ctrl+F and step between matches — all unchanged. **(4)** If it is still sluggish: Settings ▸ Diagnostics ▸ Detailed performance capture → ON, work until it happens, Help ▸ Report a problem…. Measured here with the app slowed four-fold: stalled 7.6 s of 12 before, 0.9 s after the first step, **0 after the second**; slowed six-fold, 5.6 s before the second step and 0 to 0.1 s after. **(5) NEW, and it is where the second step could show: scroll.** In a session with hundreds of messages, wheel up through the history fast, and drag the scroll bar from bottom to top. **Expected: no blank stretches, nothing flickering in, and the scroll bar the same length throughout.** The second step sets the off-screen conversation aside in pieces of about forty messages rather than one at a time, so a mistake would look like a gap the height of a screen or more. **(6)** Scroll to the middle, switch session, switch back: exactly where you were. **(7)** Switch between quiet / normal / firehose in a long session: the conversation should not end in empty space, and **Jump to latest** should still land on the last message. Covered by tests, don't repeat: the scroll height is exact at 60 and 400 messages, after a resize, and after hiding and showing tool rows inside a set-aside piece (that last one fails by a wide margin with the fix removed). **(8) NEW with the third step (2026-10-07): watch a LONG reply being written** — ask for something with headings, a numbered list, a table and a code block, several screens long. **Expected: it looks exactly as it always did while it arrives** (bold and lists forming as they are typed, the caret at the end of the last line), no paragraph jumping or changing spacing as the next one starts, and no change at the moment the reply finishes other than the Copy buttons appearing on code. The third step draws only the paragraph being written instead of the whole reply each time, so a mistake would show as a seam between paragraphs or a bit of formatting that is wrong until the reply ends. **(9)** While a long reply is still arriving, Ctrl+F for a word in its first paragraph: the highlight should appear and STAY. Measured: a 13,000-character reply still growing, app slowed six-fold, drew 240–307 frames of ~750 before and 324–690 after (better in every one of four back-to-back pairs, on a machine that was busy with something else). |
| **The plan approval bar showed the plan as one line of raw text (#1071)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1122) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | **(1)** Start a session in **plan** mode and ask for something with a few steps: the bar reads **Approve this plan?** with the plan formatted under it. **(2)** Ask for something big: the plan scrolls inside the bar and the buttons stay visible. **(3)** The buttons are **Allow**, **Deny**, **Deny with feedback…** — no **Allow all (this session)**. Say so if you want it back. **(4)** **Deny with feedback…** brings a revised plan. **(5)** **Allow**: the work starts, and its first change is on an ordinary bar that does offer Allow all. |
| **The mode chip still said "plan" after a plan was approved (#1072)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1121) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | Found while measuring plan mode, not from a hand-test. **(1)** Set a session's shield chip to **plan** and restart it: the header shows a **plan** badge. **(2)** Ask for a small change and press **Allow** on the plan bar: the badge goes away and the chip reads **ask now · plan at next start**. **(3)** Hover the chip: it starts by saying the session is in ask right now. **(4)** Restart the session: badge and chip are back on **plan**. **(5)** **Deny** a plan instead: nothing changes. |
| **Ctrl+= did not zoom (#1114)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1119) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | **Your 2026-10-05 report.** Only Ctrl+Shift+= ever worked. **(1)** Click in a prompt box and press **Ctrl+=** three times: everything grows each time, and nothing is typed. **(2)** **Ctrl+-** shrinks a step; **Ctrl+0** is back to normal in one press. **(3)** Hold **Ctrl+=**: it keeps growing and stops at about double. **(4)** Same three chords with the keyboard in an open file. **(5)** Pop a session out and zoom in the pop-out: both windows change together. **(6)** Zoom in a step, quit, reopen: same size. **(7)** The **View** menu reads Ctrl+0, Ctrl+= and Ctrl+-. ⚠️ Your install is already one step zoomed in — the shifted form worked once and the app remembered. **Ctrl+0** puts it back. |
| **A long, busy session stops following its newest message (#1111)** | **MERGED 2026-10-06 (PR #1117) — IN v0.8.115, installable now** | **Your 2026-10-05 report, and this time it was reproduced before it was fixed:** in a conversation past a thousand entries, one click anywhere in it stopped it following about one time in two. **(1)** THE MAIN ONE: pick a session that has been working a long time (hundreds of tool calls). While Claude is writing, click around in the conversation — expand a tool box, collapse it, press **Copy**, click empty space. It must keep following, and **↓ Jump to latest** must not appear. **(2)** Roll the wheel DOWN a few times at the bottom while it writes: it stays at the bottom. **(3)** Scroll UP: it stays where you put it and **↓ Jump to latest** appears. **(4)** Press it, or scroll all the way down: it follows again. **(5)** Drag the scrollbar up, and drag-select text upward past the top edge: both let you leave. **If it ever stops following without you scrolling up, send Help ▸ Report a problem… straight away** — the log now records what the view thought you did (`[feed-pin] stopped following: cause=…`). Known and not fixed: drag-selecting text in a thousand-entry conversation while it streams can still stop it following. |
| **Prompt box takes clicks and paste but not typing, until you switch apps (#909)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.97** (2026-09-21) | **Your 2026-09-21 report.** The cause was the "close this session?" question. **(1)** THE MAIN ONE: click a session tab's **✕**, press **Cancel** (or Escape), click in that session's prompt box and type. The letters must appear straight away, with no switching apps. Before the fix, nothing appeared, every time. **(2)** Same with the sessions list's close, and with **Close all sessions** from the palette: cancel it, then type. **(3)** A POPOUT: pop a session out (⤢), click **✕** on its tab inside the popout, cancel, then click into the popout's prompt and type. Say which window you end up in and whether typing works: this case was never measured. **(4)** Nothing flickers or jumps to another app when the question closes (a very brief flicker of the window behind is possible; say if you see it). **(5)** If typing ever dies again, note what you did just before; the log will say `refocus after dialog: focus did not come back` if the repair was refused. Automated: reproduced and fixed with real mouse and keyboard input on this machine (probe in `spike/probes/909/`, reverting the fix brings the bug back), unit tests on the repair and a guard that no new pop-up skips it. CI cannot run real OS input, so your hand test is the real check |
| **Report a problem: Send report looked disabled and never closed the dialog (#896)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.94** (2026-09-21) — installable now | **Your 2026-09-21 report.** Open **Help ▸ Report a problem…**. **(1)** With no subject, **Send report** is a faded green (hover it: it says to give it a subject first). **(2)** Type a subject: it turns solid green, clearly different from **Cancel**. **(3)** Pick **Just build the zip** and press **Send report**: the folder with the zip opens and the dialog CLOSES by itself. **(4)** Pick **Create a GitHub issue** and send: the new issue opens in your browser and the dialog closes (check the issue on the repo, then close it). **(5)** Unplug the network (or turn Wi-Fi off) and send to GitHub: the dialog STAYS open, the reason sits next to the button, and your subject and description are still there. **(6)** The form must not scroll sideways | Automated: unit tests for close-on-success, stay-on-failure, reason placement, the button colours and the unwritten-zip message; e2e measures the real computed fill and the sideways overflow (both proven red on the old code). The real GitHub post and the browser opening need your machine |

| **Clear Session leaves the old text until a SECOND clear (#748)** | **RELEASED in v0.8.8** (2026-09-08) — installable now | **This is your 2026-09-03 report. The one case that matters most is the one you never tried: clear a session you have JUST OPENED.** That was guaranteed broken and is the actual bug — Claude Code does not announce a conversation's identity until the session has replied once, so switchboard was comparing the new identity against nothing. **(1)** Start a fresh Direct session, and before typing anything else use **⋯ → Clear conversation**. The text must go on the FIRST clear, with the *"Conversation cleared — context starts fresh"* divider. **(2)** Same again but typed: `/clear` in the prompt box, on a session that has not replied yet. **(3)** The worst case, which failed every single time: **resume** a session with a screenful of history (quit and relaunch, or reopen a card), then clear it immediately — the replayed history must go. **(4)** The case that always worked, to prove nothing regressed: talk to a session for a few turns, then clear — one wipe, one divider, not two. **(5)** `/compact` on a session with some history — the conversation must **stay on screen**; measured not to trigger a wipe, and this is the check that it stayed that way. **(6)** Clear while Claude is mid-reply and confirm the half-written answer goes with everything else | ⚠️ **UNTESTED — released in v0.8.8 (2026-09-09); ready to run.** Root cause MEASURED against the real CLI, and **the ticket's own explanation was wrong** — it blamed a late announcement; the announcement is prompt, there was simply nothing to compare it against. Automated: 14 tests, **all 14 mutations caught**, including the real message sequence and its measured ordering. Also measured, because keying the wipe on the wrong signal would have been a worse bug: **`/compact` does not emit the reset and keeps its id**; a **`--resume` spawn emits nothing at all** (so a resumed card's replayed history is safe); and **plan-mode exit** emits nothing and does not rotate the id. **What no test can do is prove it on your machine with the real CLI** — items 1, 2 and 3 |

Hits from before the restart may say "earlier than the conversation on screen"; hits since it must jump. **Note the premise was measured wrong in the ticket:** an idle resumed session was always fine; it is the new turn on top that broke it |

Also: scroll up, switch away, come back — you should be where you were reading, not at the bottom. The restart was never the cause — clicking a card was |
| Stale **"N need you"** counters after dismissing (#621) | **MERGED to main** (PR for #621) | Let two or three sessions raise events (a permission ask, a finished turn). Read the four counts: the Events **tab**'s number, the **"N need you"** at the right of the strip across the top, and each **group header** + the **footer** of the Sessions list. Open the drawer (`Ctrl+E`) and **✕** one entry — every count must drop by one *the moment you click*, and at zero the words should vanish entirely (the group headers go back to **calm**). Then check the deliberate half: a session that is genuinely still holding a permission must KEEP its coloured, bold row in the Sessions list — dismissing is meant to clear the count, not to pretend the session is unblocked. Finally click a **Done.** entry instead of dismissing it: opening the session relaxes it to **Ready**, and that should also drop the counts | Automated: unit tests pin the derivation (dismiss, acknowledge, `none`-silenced sessions, per-group counting) and an e2e drives a real hook → real ✕ → all three rail/strip readouts. Only your eye judges whether a still-loud row next to a zero count reads right — that is the boundary with #528 |
| Dock-back slot (#558) | **FIXED, in Dan's PR queue** (PR for #558) | Your own four-step repro, with the grid split into two side-by-side sessions (A left, B right) so a wrong slot has somewhere to be wrong: **(1)** pop A out and click ⤡ — A returns to the LEFT half, not into B's tabs. **(2)** pop A out again, click ＋ inside that window to start C in it. **(3)** dock A back — A goes to the left half, alone. **(4)** dock C back — C arrives as a TAB beside A rather than taking the left half, and A is still there. Then the same in reverse (dock C first, then A): C lands beside B, A gets its left half back. Nothing should show a **Resume** button at any point, and no 1px sliver of a pane should be left in the layout. The full sequence in both orders is in `e2e/popout-dock-back.spec.ts` — the machine already checks placement and aliveness, so what is worth your eyes is whether the result FEELS right |

| 2026-09-20 | **v0.8.92, dogfooding:** the AI task label shows on the card header but the SESSIONS LIST row shows no label. **Diagnosed — already fixed by #877, merging.** Not a data bug: the row and the header read the same `taskLabel`, so a genuinely blank line is impossible there. v0.8.92's row renders `p.needsYou ? t(p.labelKey) : (s.taskLabel ?? …)` — it substitutes the STATE/ASK for the label whenever the session needs you, and `done` (finished, unreviewed) is in that set deliberately, which is the state a session sits in the moment it stops typing. So the row was reading *Finished — review changes* rather than the label. The `aria-label` dropped it too (`!p.needsYou && s.taskLabel`), so it was lost to a screen reader as well. #877 moves the state beside the NAME and gives the label its own lines, and it was filed BEFORE this report — the owner found by hand the defect the layout change was already fixing. | #877 |
